# Experiment Results 2

Plan: the stage-2 plan, as revised on 2026-11-15/16 (see §0). Generated by `report_stage2.py` from `data/results/stage2/`. All rates are seed-pooled unless marked; E_FA is the failure-adjusted error in mm.

**Arm names in this document**

| short name | arm | history | training |
|---|---|---|---|
| PID-N / PID-R | sealed fixed PIDs (250, 4.545, 0.397) / (8, 0.5, 4.4) | – | – |
| PID-RN | fixed PID retuned on the ranged validation subset (2.0, 0.5, 0.5, τd 0.01) | – | – |
| GS-N / DR-N | scheduler / direct, nominal sampler | H = 1 | new (F1) |
| GS-D / DR-D | scheduler / direct, ranged sampler | H = 1 | new (main) |
| GS-N H10 / DR-N H10 | sealed nominal checkpoints | H = 10 | sealed |
| GS-D H10 / DR-D H10 | ranged sampler | H = 10 | new (history ablation) |
| GS-D / DR-D old-sampler H10 | sealed disturbed checkpoints | H = 10 | sealed |
| GS-D / DR-D old-sampler H1 | E5 checkpoints (old sampler) | H = 1 | results 1, E5 |

### 0.1 Deviations from the stage-2 plan

1. **Design revised with the author (2026-11-15/16) before any run:**
   - The disturbed arms GS-D and DR-D train on a ranged sampler: delay uniform in [0, 0.3] s and noise
     log-uniform in [0.1, 10] mm, with no point mass at the declared severity.
   - Checkpoints are selected on `validation_ranged.json`.
   - The main table uses a new ranged held-out set (`held_out_ranged.json`). The fixed held-out set is kept
     as a comparison (§3.4).
   - F7 (noise-trained H = 1 arms) is therefore part of the main design (§8).
2. **H = 10 ablation of the disturbed arms was retrained** with the same ranged sampler (`GS-D H10`, `DR-D H10`)
   instead of reusing the sealed H = 10 checkpoints. Otherwise an H1 − H10 difference would also contain the
   sampler change.
   - The nominal H = 10 arms are the sealed nominal checkpoints; the nominal sampler is unchanged.
   - The sealed disturbed checkpoints and the E5 H = 1 checkpoints (both old sampler) are reported as references.
3. **PID-RN** was retuned on the ranged validation subset by the E4 grid procedure. It took three rounds:
   - round 1 selected Kp 4.0 at the lower Kp edge;
   - round 2 (Kp down to 1) selected Kd 0.5 and τd 0.01, both at their lower edges;
   - round 3 (Kd down to 0, τd down to 0.005) selected the same point. Final: (2.0, 0.5, 0.5, τd 0.01), §0.2.
   - The round-1 PID-RN rows inside the job-3225586 directories are not used.
4. **Models** for all new arms are in `data/checkpoints/`, not `f1_history1_nominal/`.
5. **F3:**
   - Every level (0–0.8 s) was run for every arm; no early stop.
   - The existing arms were re-run rather than reusing E2 rows. Their 2,640 rows at the sealed level
     (0.15 s) reproduce the sealed run bit for bit (§0).
6. **F4:**
   - Built as a scenario manifest with multiple delay events; `env.py` was not changed.
   - A combined variant (dynamic noise on its own schedule) was added at the author's request.
   - Zero-command counts are computed from the schedules (§5).
7. **F5:** PID-N, PID-R, GS-N H10 and DR-N H10 rows are the E1 rows from results 1 (same scenarios and
   controller instances), not re-runs.
8. **F6:** frozen gains come from the sealed `validation.json` (as in E3 and the plan), and are evaluated on
   the ranged held-out set only.
9. **F0:** read from the training logs; no replay. The ranged arms were validated on `validation_ranged.json`,
   all others on `validation.json`, so validation numbers are comparable only within those groups.
10. **Identical cells:** in both held-out sets the transient-nominal scenarios carry no event, so they
    reproduce the stationary-nominal cells exactly. The two nominal columns are identical by construction.

| round (grid) | candidates | selected (Kp, Ki, Kd) | on grid edge | val compl % | val E_FA mm | steer TV/T |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (Kp 4–40, Kd 0.5–8, τd 0.01–0.1) | 224 | (4, 0.5, 3, τd 0.02) | kp | 100 | 15.2 | 74.1 |
| 2 (Kp 1–40, Kd 0.5–8, τd 0.01–0.1) | 392 | (2, 0.5, 0.5, τd 0.01) | kd, tau_d | 100 | 12.0 | 32.3 |
| 3 (Kp 1.5–3, Kd 0–1, τd 0.005–0.02) | 49 | (2, 0.5, 0.5, τd 0.01) | none | 100 | 12.0 | 32.3 |

- **Validation subset:** the 28-scenario robust subset of `validation_ranged.json`. Selection rule: `calibrate_pid.select_practical_candidate`.
- **Sealed PID-R (8, 0.5, 4.4) on the same subset:** 82.1 % completion, 23.2 mm.
- **Final PID-RN:** the rule applied to all candidates of the three rounds together selects the round-2/3 point, which is used everywhere below.

- **Validation:** completion % / E_FA mm of the checkpoint each run kept. Checkpoint scenarios are the 24 validation scenarios that are stationary-nominal or transient-combined.
- **Held-out, same cells:** the mean of the held-out stationary-nominal and transient-combined cells. Ranged arms use the ranged held-out set; arms selected on `validation.json` use the fixed held-out set.

- **E_FA, H = 1 vs H = 10 on validation:** in all six pairs, validation and held-out favour the same history
  setting.
  - H = 1 has the lower E_FA for GS-D, DR-D, DR-N, GS-D old-sampler and DR-D old-sampler.
  - H = 10 has the lower E_FA for GS-N: 24.7 vs 28.6 mm on validation, 23.7 vs 27.0 mm on held-out.
- **Completion:**
  - GS-N and GS-D differ by under 1 pp on validation, in H = 10's favour, and by 0.5 / 3.0 pp on held-out,
    in H = 1's favour.
  - DR-D: 100 % (H = 1) vs 97.5 % (H = 10) on validation. The gap comes from DR-D H10 seed 37: 87.5 %,
    205 mm.
- **GS-D (ranged) has seed-mean validation completion of 84.2 %** (seeds 75.0–100 %). Its held-out completion on
  the same cell types is 94.0 %.
- **One-line answer:** validation points the same way as the held-out result for every H1/H10 pair on E_FA.
  On completion, GS-N and GS-D differ in direction by ≤ 3 pp.

## 4. F3 Constant-delay sweep, 0–0.8 s

**Training disturbance sampler as implemented** (`env.py::_sample_disturbance`, `config.py`).

- **Condition per episode** (both disturbed samplers): nominal 25 %, delay 25 %, noise 15 %, combined 35 %.
- **Timing:** each disturbed episode is stationary (disturbance from t = 0) with p = 0.5. Otherwise it is
  transient, with 1, 2 or 3 changes (p = 0.50 / 0.30 / 0.20) at times uniform in
  [1 s, max(1 s, 0.8 · ideal duration)]. Each change after the first returns the channel to zero with p = 0.35;
  otherwise it draws a new value. In combined episodes delay and noise change at the same times.
- **Delay value per draw:**
  - old sampler (sealed GS-D/DR-D, E5 H1): 0.15 s with p = 0.5, otherwise uniform [0, 0.15] s.
    Mean 0.1125 s, maximum 0.15 s;
  - ranged sampler (stage 2 GS-D, DR-D, GS-D H10, DR-D H10): uniform [0, 0.3] s, no point mass. Mean 0.15 s.
- **Noise value per draw:**
  - old sampler: 0.3 mm with p = 0.5, otherwise uniform [0, 0.4] mm;
  - ranged sampler: log-uniform [0.1, 10] mm.
- **Quantisation:** delays are applied in whole physics steps (`round(delay / DT)`).
- **Nominal arms** (GS-N, DR-N, both H) never see a disturbance in training.

Levels of the F3 sweep inside the training range:
- ranged arms: 0–0.3 s (levels 0, 0.03, 0.06, 0.1, 0.15, 0.2, 0.3);
- old-sampler arms: 0–0.15 s;
- nominal arms: 0 only. PID gains are fixed.


Delay only, noise off. Stationary: delay from t = 0. Transient: step at 6 s. 60 scenarios per mode and level. Every level was run for every arm; no early stop.

## 5. F4 Dynamic delay

**Schedule** (`make_test_scenarios.py dynamic`, seed 20261116, identical for every arm).

- **Base scenarios:** the 60 held-out stationary delay records (20 paths × 3 speeds), noise off.
- **Delay:** the initial value is drawn uniform in [0, D_max] at t = 0. Each value is held for a time drawn
  uniform in [1 s, 3 s]; the next value is drawn uniform in [0, D_max]. This repeats up to the episode time limit
  (2 × ideal duration + 5 s).
- **Ranges:** D_max = 0.15 s (the old training range), 0.3 s (the ranged training range) and 0.45 s.
- **Combined variant:** the same delay schedule, plus noise on its own schedule. Holds are drawn uniform in
  [1 s, 3 s]; values log-uniform in [0.1, 10] mm.
- **Delay buffer** (unchanged, `env.py::_set_delay_steps`):
  - when the delay grows, the queue is padded with copies of its oldest command, or with zero commands if
    the queue is empty;
  - when it shrinks, the oldest commands are dropped;
  - at t = 0 every episode with a non-zero initial delay starts with that many zero commands queued. This is
    the same as in the sealed run.

**How it differs from training.** The ranged training sampler changes the delay 1–3 times in half of its
delay-bearing episodes, at times spread over the first 80 % of the ideal duration, and with returns to zero.
F4 changes it every 1–3 s through the whole episode (counts below), with no forced return to zero.


**Change counts and zero-command intervals**

| D_max | changes per scenario to the time limit, mean (range) | scenarios starting at 0 steps | 0-step → >0 changes (to time limit) | … before the median episode end |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.15 s | 20.2 (11–32) | 1 | 6 | 2 |
| 0.3 s | 20.2 (12–31) | 0 | 1 | 0 |
| 0.45 s | 20.2 (12–34) | 0 | 4 | 1 |

- **Change counts** run up to the episode time limit; an episode that finishes early sees fewer.
- **"0-step → >0" changes** are the cases where the delay grows from an empty queue, so the first new slots are filled with zero commands.
- **The last column** keeps only changes before the median finish time over all arms and seeds on that scenario.

  - PID-RN completes 100 % (TV/T 262 1/s, saturation 36 %);
  - DR-D and DR-N complete 100 % (saturation 11 % and 6 %);
  - PID-R completes 0 %; GS-D 2 %; GS-N 4 %.
- **Noise + 0.15 s delay:**
  - GS-N is below 100 % already at 0.3 mm (pooled 76.2 %);
  - PID-N is at 0 % at every level;
  - DR-D breaks at 54.8 mm (E_FA rule).

- **GS-D (H = 1, ranged), delay-bearing cells:** mean completion is 88.2 % with dynamic gains, 89.4 % with
  F-clean and 91.0 % with F-delay.
  - Seed 71 is flagged: dynamic 95.8 % vs F-delay 82.5 % and F-clean 73.8 %.
  - Seed 53 goes the other way: dynamic 69.2 % vs frozen 96.7 / 96.2 %.
- **GS-N (H = 1):** mean 59.6 % dynamic vs 55.9 / 54.9 % frozen. Seed 23 is flagged: dynamic 44.6 % vs F-delay
  33.8 % and F-clean 15.8 %. Its F-clean Kp is 207.3, against 18–39 for the other seeds.
- **Frozen GS-D gains:** F-clean and F-delay Kp differ by 0.1–2.3 between the two operating points of a seed.
  Kp ranges 5.1–11.8 across seeds.
- **Compared with results 1 (E3, H = 10, old sampler):** there the flagged GS-D seed was 23. Here the flagged
  GS-D seed is 71, and seed 23 loses nothing when frozen.

## 8. F7 Noise-trained H = 1 arms

Merged into the main design. With the author's revision, the main disturbed arms (GS-D, DR-D) are trained on noise log-uniform in [0.1, 10] mm (together with delay uniform in [0, 0.3] s), and PID-RN is the noise-aware PID baseline. A separate σ* = 3 mm H = 1 run was not made.

## 9. Observations

Facts per section are written under each section above (F0–F6). No interpretation.

## 10. Files

- **Results:** `data/results/stage2/<step>/` (`episodes.csv`, `summary.csv`, `summary_by_seed.csv`, `metadata.json`).
- **Models:** `data/checkpoints/`.
- **Manifests:** `data/scenarios/{held_out_ranged,validation_ranged,dynamic,delay_wide}.json`.
- **Code:** `make_test_scenarios.py`, `validation_scores.py`, `tune_pid_rn.py`, `report_stage2.py`, `figures_stage2.py`.
- **Jobs:** `cluster/stage2_range_training.sh`, `cluster/stage2b_pid_rn.sh`.

**Figures** (`data/results/stage2/figures/`, PDF + 300 dpi PNG). Main-arm colours are fixed across figures, with a marker and line style per arm (PID square/dashed, PID-RN diamond, scheduler circle/solid, direct triangle/dotted; nominal hollow, disturbed filled).

| file | content |
|---|---|
| `f2_heldout_main` | F2: completion and E_FA of the seven main arms in every ranged held-out cell. |
| `f2_history_paired_difference` | F2: paired ΔE_FA, H = 1 − H = 10, per arm and cell (seed-mean, 95 % CI, seeds). |
| `f3_delay_sweep` | F3: completion and E_FA vs constant delay 0–0.8 s, main arms; training ranges shaded. |
| `f4_dynamic_delay` | F4: completion and E_FA vs dynamic-delay range D_max, delay-only and combined. |
| `f5_noise_sweep_stationary` | F5: completion and E_FA vs noise, stationary, main arms. |
| `f5_noise_sweep_transient` | F5: completion and E_FA vs noise, transient, main arms. |
| `f5_mechanism_stationary_noise` | F5: steering TV/T and wheel saturation vs noise (stationary, noise only). |
| `f6_frozen_gain_completion` | F6: delay-cell completion per seed, dynamic vs frozen gains (H = 1 schedulers). |
